<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which molecule is the sulfonate donor associated with the binding-site task for this sulfotransferase?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Thyroxine<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Adenosine 3′,5′-bisphosphate<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Inorganic sulfate<br><br><strong>D.</strong> 3′-Phosphoadenylyl sulfate (PAPS)<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: The question asks about the sulfonate donor molecule for a sulfotransferase enzyme. Sulfotransferases are a well-characterized enzyme family that catalyze the transfer of a sulfonate (sulfo) group to acceptor substrates. The universal sulfonate donor for ALL sulfotransferases is 3&#x27;-Phosphoadenylyl sulfate (PAPS, also known as 3&#x27;-phosphoadenosine-5&#x27;-phosphosulfate). This is a fundamental biochemical fact.<br><br>Evaluating each option:<br>- A. Thyroxine: This is a thyroid hormone that can serve as an acceptor substrate for certain sulfotransferases (e.g., tyrosylprotein sulfotransferase), not a donor.<br>- B.
